# 08 The plan rulebook: framework (to be filled from the founder's research)

Status: framework only, 2026-10-11. No values below are decided except those marked FIXED (founder rules already given). Every value marked TO SET comes from the founder's independent research and is approved by the founder before any code is written. Nothing in the app changes until then.

## Part 1. How the rulebook governs every plan

1. **One rulebook, in one place.** The rulebook becomes a single data module in the app: category structures, numeric limits and the rule list. Every value carries its source. The planner reads it and hard-codes no structure or limit of its own.
2. **One checker, run on every plan the app can produce.** A test builds every combination (every category, 2 to 6 days, every session length, every equipment setting, focus picks, experience levels) with the real exercise library. It also runs every path that changes a plan after it is built: weekly check-ins, swaps, rebuilds and continuity, library and kit plans, and the older generator wherever it can still run. Any rule broken fails the gate, and nothing merges.
3. **Founder sign-off before any build.** A rendered page shows every category's week at every day count, with its measured checks. The founder approves it before an EAS build is started.
4. **Change control.** Changing a rulebook value needs a source and the founder's approval. There is no same-day patch-and-ship: every change runs the full checker first.

## Part 2. FIXED rules (founder decisions already given)

| # | Rule | Source |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | The coaching engine is deterministic: same inputs, same plan. No AI or randomness at runtime. | CLAUDE.md section 2 |
| F2 | The category's standard routine is always kept. Focus picks and emphasis add on top and never take from it. | Founder, 2026-10-10 |
| F3 | No exercise or set is removed to fit the clock. Rest is shortened first, then the real session length is shown. | Founder, 2026-10-10 |
| F4 | Men's physique trains legs in one session a week at every day count. | Founder, 2026-10-10 |
| F5 | Users have no fixed training days. The next session in the rotation may be the next calendar day. | Founder, D219 (2026-10-04) |
| F6 | A plan with poor structure that risks injury is unacceptable. Recovery is never traded for volume. | Founder, 2026-10-11 |

## Part 3. The decisions the research must answer (TO SET)

### A. Category week structures

For each category (general, bodybuilding, men's physique, classic physique, bikini, wellness, figure, women's physique) and each day count (2 to 6):

| Category | Days | Session 1 | Session 2 | Session 3 | Session 4 | Session 5 | Session 6 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (each) | (2 to 6) | name: muscles | ... | | | | | TO SET |

- A1: May the order of sessions be changed by the planner, or is it fixed? Which pairs must never be adjacent?
- A2: May a muscle not named in a session be added to it, for example to reach a set target? If yes, under what limit?

### B. Weekly volume per muscle, per category

| Category | Muscle | Minimum direct sets a week | Typical | Upper | Sessions a week | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (each) | (each) | TO SET | TO SET | TO SET | TO SET | |

- B1: How indirect work counts toward a muscle (for example, does a chest press count as part of a triceps or front-delt set, and at what fraction?).
- B2: Starting volume in week 1 and the climb to the peak week, and the deload week.

### C. Recovery between sessions

| Muscle | Minimum hours before training it directly again | With heavy compound load | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| (each) | TO SET | TO SET | |

- C1: Does indirect load (for example front delts from chest pressing) count against these hours?
- C2: The rule when the next session may be the next day (F5): is it "no muscle trained directly in two back-to-back sessions", or something weaker for fast-recovering muscles (calves, abs, side delts)?

### D. Session limits

| Limit | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| D1 Exercises per session, maximum | TO SET | |
| D2 Working sets per session, maximum | TO SET | |
| D3 Sets per exercise, compound and isolation, maximum | TO SET | |
| D4 Direct sets per muscle in one session, maximum | TO SET | |
| D5 Exercises per muscle in one session, maximum | TO SET | |
| D6 What happens when the standard does not fit the chosen session length (F3 says nothing is removed; the research says how the week should be shaped instead) | TO SET | |

### E. Joint protection

| Rule | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| E1 Overhead pressing: sessions a week, maximum | TO SET | |
| E2 Pressing (chest or overhead) in back-to-back sessions | TO SET | |
| E3 Heavy hinge and heavy squat: back-to-back sessions | TO SET | |
| E4 Elbow load: arm isolation in back-to-back sessions | TO SET | |
| E5 Front delts: trained directly at all where chest pressing already loads them? | TO SET | |

### F. Exercise selection

- F-1: Exercises per muscle per session and across the week; how much variety is required.
- F-2: The same exercise in two back-to-back sessions: allowed or not.
- F-3: Equipment fallbacks when a muscle has only one exercise for the person's kit, and whether one exercise may then take more sets than the normal cap.
- F-4: Exercise order inside a session (compounds first, focus muscle first, and so on).

### G. Focus picks

- G1: How many sets a focus pick adds, and in which sessions.
- G2: What a focus pick may never do: break C, D or E, or take from another muscle's standard (F2).

### H. Priority order when goals conflict

An ordered list, most important first, of: recovery between sessions (C), joint protection (E), the category standard (B minimum), focus additions (G), session limits (D), session length (F3), growth above the standard. Each with a reason and a source.

### I. Experience levels

How beginner and advanced change B, C, D and the climb.

### J. After the plan is built

Weekly check-ins (raising, holding, pulling back sets), swaps, rebuilds and library plans must all obey A to H. For each: TO SET what it may change and what it may never change.

## Part 4. The checker: rules in checkable form

Each rule becomes one check over every plan. The numbers come from Part 3.

| Check | Fails when | Values from |
|---|---|---|
| K1 Structure | A session's muscles differ from the category's table for that day count | A |
| K2 Volume | A muscle's weekly direct sets fall outside its category range at the peak week | B |
| K3 Recovery | A muscle is trained directly in two sessions closer than its minimum hours, assuming the next session may be the next day | C, F5 |
| K4 Session limits | A session exceeds an exercise, set, per-muscle or per-exercise limit | D |
| K5 Joint protection | An overhead, pressing, hinge or elbow rule is broken | E |
| K6 Variety | The same exercise appears in two back-to-back sessions, or two sessions are near-copies | F |
| K7 Focus | A focus pick takes sets from another muscle's standard, or breaks K3 to K5 | G |
| K8 Determinism | The same inputs give a different plan | F1 |
| K9 Length | A session is past the chosen length without the plan saying so | F3 |
| K10 After the build | A check-in, swap, rebuild or library plan produces a plan that fails K1 to K9 | J |

# 03 Real samples from the live planner (generated 2026-10-11)

Every plan below was produced by the live planner code on `main`, with the real seeded exercise library, by `tools/generate-samples.jest.js` in this folder. Nothing was typed by hand. Inputs not listed: first block, no logged history, average recovery rating, no learned factor, no continuity, not strength phase. "Usual gap" is `TYPICAL_WEEK_GAP_HOURS` in src/lib/recovery/constants.js (4 days: Mon, Tue, Thu, Fri). Users have no fixed training days, so any back-to-back pair can fall on consecutive days.

## A. Men's physique, 4 days, 60 min, focus side delts and biceps, with all six weeks

This is the structure behind the founder's plan of 10 October (screenshots in the conversation: Legs + Abs, Upper A (Width), Shoulders + Arms, Upper B (Detail)). The founder's own plan shows different exercise names (machine chest press, hack squat, Smith machine Romanian deadlift) because the live app's catalogue prefers exercises the person has logged and the rebuild keeps the person's existing exercises (`plannerV2KeptChoices`, `src/lib/exercise/continuity.js`), and a different served order because the person's recovery rating, learned recovery factor and own gaps between sessions change the order (`personalise` in `src/lib/plan/planner.js`). This sample has no logged history, so it shows the catalogue's first choices and the population order.

### mens_physique, 4 days, 60 min, full_gym, intermediate, focus side_delts + biceps

Plan name: V-Taper 4×/week. Structure: `division_mens_physique_4`. Served order: Upper A (Width), Legs + Abs, Shoulders + Arms, Upper B (Detail).
Readiness check passes: **false**. Limited muscles (lowest estimated recovery before a session): side_delts 37%, biceps 40%, triceps 50%, rear_delts 62%.
Sessions past the chosen length by more than 5 minutes at the peak: Upper A (Width) +29.5 min, Legs + Abs +22.5 min, Upper B (Detail) +16 min, Shoulders + Arms +13.5 min. Sessions past 8 exercises or 25 sets: Upper A (Width), Legs + Abs, Upper B (Detail), Shoulders + Arms.

**1. Upper A (Width)** (about 90 min at the peak week; next session follows after 24 h in the app's usual layout)

| # | Exercise | Muscle | Kind | Sets week 1 | Sets week 5 (peak) | Reps | Rest s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

# 06 Division research: Men's Physique and Classic Physique

Research date: 2026-10-10. Method: every claim below was read on a page fetched this session; the URL is given inline. Where a fact could not be fetched, it says NOT FOUND. Search snippets were used only to locate pages, never as evidence.

Evidence grades used throughout:
- PRIMARY = words or programme by the athlete or coach (own site, own quoted video).
- SECONDARY = a magazine or site reporting or reconstructing the athlete's routine (named, but not their own document).
- UNATTRIBUTED = no named coach or athlete (kept only to show the tally; excluded from "named source" counts).

Important limits, stated up front:
- Almost no PRIMARY programme documents were reachable (Muscle and Strength returned HTTP 403 on every fetch; Sommerfeld's own programme page only advertises a paid PDF). Most splits below are SECONDARY reconstructions and several are contest-prep, not off-season. Each row says which.
- ifbbpro.com was never returned by search or fetched. The "IFBB Pro League" criteria below are NOT FOUND; the IFBB (federation, ifbb.com) rule PDFs and the NPC pages were used instead.

---

## 1. Judging criteria

### 1.1 Men's Physique

**NPC** (https://npcnewsonline.com/official-npc-mens-physique-division/), "Contest Format (Judging)":
- "Judges will be looking for fit competitors who display proper shape and symmetry" combined with "muscularity and overall condition."
- "This is not a bodybuilding contest so extreme muscularity will be marked down."
- "Judges will also be looking for the competitor with the best stage presence and poise."
- The page's judging text does not mention legs or lower body. Board shorts appear only in General Rules: "They must be one inch below the navel." "They must extend to two inches above the kneecap and be tapered." "Spandex is prohibited."

**IFBB federation, Section 9 Men's Physique 2017** (https://ifbb.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Mens-Physique-2017.pdf, text extracted from the PDF):
- Art. 10.1: assessment "should begin at the head and extend downwards, taking the whole physique into account."
- "Judges will be looking for fit contestants who display proper shape and body proportions, combined with balanced muscularity and overall condition. The judges are reminded that extreme muscularity and definition should be marked down."
- Appendix 2 (legs, explicit): "With the exception of the upper legs, which are covered by board shorts, the assessment should take the whole physique into account."
- Appendix 2: "The judges should favour competitors with a harmonious, proportional, classical male physique, good posture, correct anatomical structure (... straight legs, not bandy or knock- kneed). Vertical proportions (legs to upper body length) and horizontal proportions (hips and waist to shoulder width) are ones of the key factors."
- Art. 6.1 shorts: "The shorts will cover the whole upper leg, down the upper point of the knee (patella)." No tight lycra.
- The 2021 edition (https://ifbb.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/04/Mens-Physique-Rules-2021-.pdf) carries identical Appendix 2 wording ("With the exception of the upper legs, which are covered by board shorts...") and the same "extreme muscularity and definition should be marked down" line. It also introduces a separate "Muscular Men's Physique" category "for competitors with slightly bigger muscularity".

**2Bros / UK pro-qualifier federation** (https://www.2bpevents.com/division-rules/):
- "fit contestants who display proper shape and symmetry combined with muscularity and overall condition." "This is not a bodybuilding contest so extreme muscularity will be marked down."
- Shorts: "Shorts must be just above the knee in length and can be one inch below the belly button." "no spandex and no logos".
- The page sets no separate leg criteria and names no muscle groups.

**Observed disagreement on shorts length:** NPC "two inches above the kneecap"; 2Bros "just above the knee"; IFBB "down to the upper point of the knee". All three cover the quads; none scores them.

### 1.2 Classic Physique

**NPC** (https://npcnewsonline.com/classic-physique/):
- "Judges shall score competitors according to the 'total package', which is a balance of size, symmetry, and muscularity."
- "During the call-outs, judges shall have the opportunity to compare competitors against each other."
- Mandatory poses: Front Double Biceps, Side Chest, Back Double Biceps, Abdominals and Thighs, Favorite Classic Pose (no Most Muscular).
- Trunks: "black trunks made from cloth fabric." The page has no sentence on physique ideal, legs, waist or shoulders.

**IFBB federation Classic Physique rules 2018** (https://ifbb.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Classic-Physique-Rules-2018.pdf, text extracted):
- Art. 7.1: judges assess "the degree of proportion, symmetry, muscle size and quality (density, separation, definition) as well as skin tone."
- Survey order: "starting from the head, and looking at every part of the physique in a downward sequence... looking for muscular bulk, balanced development, muscular density and definition."
- "The downward survey should take in the head, neck, shoulders, chest, all of the arm muscles, front of the trunk for pectorals, pec-delt tie-in, abdominals, waist, thighs, legs and calves and feet." Back poses add "the upper and lower trapezius, teres and infraspinatus, erector spinae, the gluteus group, the leg biceps group at the back of the thighs and calves and feet."
- Art. 10.2: "The judge should favour competitors with a harmonious, classical physique... correct anatomical structure (including body framework, broad shoulders, high chest, correct spinal curves, limbs and trunk in good proportion, straight legs...)."
- Attire is gymnastic shorts "allowing to display the upper legs" (so legs are fully visible and named in the survey, unlike Men's Physique).

**2Bros** (https://www.2bpevents.com/division-rules/): judging "scored 100%", five mandatory poses as above; "Abdominal and Thighs" is the only mandatory pose involving the legs; "You are NOT to wear board shorts or Bodybuilding posing trunks." No muscle groups named.

NOT FOUND: any ifbbpro.com page for either division; any Classic Physique height/weight table (not requested, not fetched).

Research context (not a coach programme): a survey of 154 competitive physique athletes, 50 Men's Physique and 26 Classic Physique (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/, same article as https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full), states: "In off-season most physique athletes use split routines (85.5%)" and "train 4-7 times per week", "target major muscle groups twice weekly". The page notes that passage has no citation, so it may be background literature, not a survey result.

---

## 3. Modal structures

Counting rule: one count per named athlete (reconstruction and own-quote pages for the same person count once). Only sources with a fetched day-level split or an explicit weekly frequency are counted. Most are SECONDARY.

### 3.1 Men's Physique

Legs per week, by athlete (fetched evidence): Hendrickson 1 (M1, M1b), Buendia 1 (M2), Bilal 1 maintenance (M6) or 0 dedicated (M6b), Grant 2 (M3), Terry 2 (M5, M5b; but M5d says 1 or 2), Hanson 2 at lower volume (M7), Cook 2 per 8 days or 1 per 3 days (M4). Result: **once = 3 (Hendrickson, Buendia, Bilal); twice = 3 (Grant, Terry, Hanson); Cook is both depending on split.** The sources disagree evenly; the six-day and four-day splits are the twice group, the five-day body-part splits are the once group.

| Days | Modal structure found | Count | Per-muscle exposures per week |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | NOT FOUND as a weekly Men's Physique template. Only Cook's rotation (Chest and back; Legs; Shoulders and arms, repeating, M4) | 1 (a rotation, not a Mon-Sun week) | each 1 per 3 days |
| 4 | Upper / Lower / rest / Upper / Lower (Hanson, M7). Bilal's 4-5 day upper-focused split (M6b) is the only other 4-day-leaning source and has no dedicated leg day | 1 programme (plus 1 near-match) | upper muscles 2, legs 2 at lower volume |
| 5 | Body-part: Chest, Legs, Shoulders, (rest), Back, Arms (Hendrickson M1, Buendia M2 in a different order: C, S, B, L, A; Mikolo M9 UNATTRIBUTED). Minorities: Grant (C; Legs+Tri; Back+Bi; Shoulders+Chest; Legs; legs 2x) and Bilal (S; B; C+A; S; B plus a sixth light leg day) | 2 named of the first kind; 1 Grant; 1 Bilal | first kind: all 1x, legs 1x. Grant: chest 2, legs 2, others 1. Bilal: back 2, delts 2, chest 1, arms 1, legs 1 or 0 |
| 6 | Push / Pull / Legs twice (Terry M5b, Cook M4, Buendia M2b unclear) or body-part with legs split into quads and hamstrings+calves (Terry M5) | 2 PPL x2 named (Terry, Cook), 1 body-part (Terry M5) | PPL x2: all 2x, legs 2x. Body-part: back, arms, shoulders, chest 1x, legs 2x |

Session list for the modal 5-day (Hendrickson, M1): Mon Chest; Tue Legs; Wed Shoulders; Thu Rest; Fri Back; Sat Arms; Sun Rest.
Session list for the modal 6-day PPL (M5b): Push (chest, shoulders, triceps); Pull (back, rear delts, biceps); Legs (quads, hamstrings, calves); repeat.

### 3.2 Classic Physique

Legs per week, by athlete: Bumstead 2 sessions per 8-9 day cycle (quad day plus hamstring day; C1, C1b, C1c, C1d), Kalecinski hamstrings 2, quads 1 (C2) or legs 1 in core split (C2b), Ruffin 3 (C3), Vissers 2 (C4, C4b), Ansley 2 (C5, inferred from "each muscle group twice"), Dino 1 (C6), editorial guide 2 (C7). **Two or more leg exposures = 6 of 7 (Bumstead, Kalecinski hamstrings, Ruffin, Vissers, Ansley, C7); once = 1 (Dino).** Where legs are twice, they are consistently split into a quad-dominant day and a hamstring-dominant day (Bumstead, Vissers, C7, Kalecinski).

| Days | Modal structure found | Count | Per-muscle exposures per week |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | NOT FOUND | 0 | none |
| 4 | NOT FOUND for an off-season split. Bumstead says "four, three to four workouts a week" during a re-entry phase (C1e) with no day list | 0 usable | none |
| 5 | Body-part: Chest, Back, Legs, Shoulders, Arms (Dino C6; prep season). Equivalent to about 5.25 sessions per 7 days in Bumstead's 8-day cycle (6 sessions per 8 days) | 1 (Dino) plus Bumstead cycle by approximation | Dino: all 1x, legs 1x |
| 6 | Chest+Abs; Back (width); Shoulders+Abs; Legs (quads); Arms; Back (thickness)+Hamstrings; Rest (C7). Kalecinski's six sessions (C2) and Vissers 2026 six-day-plus-rest pattern (C4b) have the same shape | C7, C2, C4b | C7: back 2, legs 2, chest/shoulders/arms 1. C2: back 2, shoulders 2, biceps 2, triceps 3, chest 1, hams 2, quads 1 |
| 7-8 day cycle | Bumstead 3-on-1-off (C1 to C1d): Quads+Calves; Chest; Back; Rest; Shoulders; Hamstrings+Back; Arms; Rest | 1 athlete, 4 pages | Per 8 days: legs 2 sessions, chest 2, back 2, biceps 2, triceps 2, shoulders 1 |

Point of disagreement worth noting: for Bumstead, shoulders appear once per cycle in all three reconstructions (C1, C1b, C1c) while the Fitness Volt page text and the Sporthiatus page text each claim a different overall frequency than their own tables show (C1 says twice weekly; C1b says most muscles once per cycle with back the exception). The day tables, not the prose, were used.

Caveat on phase: Vissers (C4b), Ansley (C5), Dino (C6), Ruffin upper-body (C3b) and Bumstead 2023 (C1d, 14 weeks out) are prep-period descriptions; Ruffin C3, Bumstead C1 and Vissers C4 are the closest to off-season.

---

## 4. Per-muscle weekly direct set ranges

### 4.1 Survey medians, off-season (largest sample)

Source: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/ (Tables 4 and 6), identical values read from https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full. Cell = weekly sets, central value (min to max). Samples: Men's Physique n = 50, Classic Physique n = 26, Bodybuilding Classic n = 14 (Frontiers page). One read described the central value as median; the other noted the table footnote labels it "mean". Treat as "central value", not a recommendation. Self-reported by a social-media-recruited sample (data collected April to December 2020).

| Muscle | Men's Physique | Classic Physique |
|---|---|---|
| Pectoral | 32 (8 to 70) | 27 (4 to 80) |
| Latissimus dorsi | 32 (8 to 70) | 29 (12 to 65) |
| Deltoid | 33.5 (6 to 80) | 26 (6 to 62) |
| Biceps brachii | 18 (0 to 32) | 16 (3 to 36) |
| Triceps brachii | 16 (4 to 40) | 16 (4 to 36) |
| Quadriceps | 22 (1 to 56) | 24 (8 to 42) |
| Hamstrings | 19 (8 to 50) | 19 (8 to 36) |
| Gluteal | 9 (0 to 30) | 12 (0 to 45) |
| Triceps surae | 16 (4 to 50) | 20 (0 to 70) |
| Abdominals | 15 (0 to 50) | 9 (0 to 50) |
| Lower back | 4 (0 to 40) | 3.5 (0 to 12) |

### 4.2 Individual athletes (sets counted from the cited page, my tally unless the page states it)

| Division | Athlete / source | Muscle (sets per week) | URL |
|---|---|---|---|
| MP | Hendrickson (BarBend, page states) | back 29, shoulders 28, each once weekly | https://barbend.com/news/bodybuilder-brandon-hendrickson-v-taper-workout/ |
| MP | Hendrickson (Jacked Gorilla, tally) | chest 21 (7 exercises x 3), legs 29 (9 exercises, one drop set), shoulders 22, back 20 to 24, arms about 38 (triceps 16, biceps 22) | https://jackedgorilla.com/brandon-hendrickson-workout-routine/ |
| MP | Grant (tally) | legs about 51 over 2 sessions (26 + 25, incl. calves 8), chest 39 (26 + 13), back 32, shoulders 23 (+ shrug 4), triceps and biceps 4-ish each | https://barbend.com/news/bodybuilder-charjo-grant-week-of-training/ |
| MP | Terry (Jacked Gorilla, page states about 125 sets total) | back 22, shoulders 19, chest 15, quads 13, hamstrings + calves 17, triceps 10, biceps 10 | https://jackedgorilla.com/ryan-terry-workout-routine/ |
| MP | Terry (Male Optimal) | push day 15-18 direct sets across 5-7 exercises; no per-muscle figure | https://www.maleoptimal.co.uk/guides/ryan-terry-training-programme |
| MP | Bilal | lateral delts "at least 15-20 direct working sets per week" (page says most do only 6-8); other muscles no figure | https://alibilalofficial.com/blog/training.html |
| MP | Buendia (Dr Workout) | chest 16 (4 x 4), legs 20 (4 x 5), back 24 (6 x 4), shoulders 24 (6 x 4), arms 4-7 sets x 7 exercises | https://www.drworkout.fitness/jeremy-buendia-workout-routine/ |
| CP | Bumstead (Kyle Hunt, per 9 days) | chest 26, back 29, biceps 30, triceps 25, shoulders 15, rear delts 7, quads 15-16, hamstrings 9, glutes 6, calves 6, adductors 6 | https://kylehuntfitness.com/chris-bumstead-workout-and-diet-program-for-2024/ |
| CP | Bumstead (Fitness Volt, per 8 days) | quads about 10, calves 3, hamstrings 6 curls + 2 deadlift, chest about 13 per session, back about 12, shoulders about 11, triceps about 8, biceps about 9 | https://fitnessvolt.com/chris-bumstead-classic-physique-training/ |
| CP | Dino (page totals per session, each once weekly) | chest 14, back 19, legs 20, shoulders 23, arms 22 | https://generationiron.com/ramon-dino-workout/ |
| CP | Editorial guide C7 (per session) | chest 13 (4+3+3+3), back width 13, shoulders 15 + 4 rear, legs quad day 13, back/ham day 18 | https://generationiron.com/classic-physique-training-split/ |
| CP | Vissers (C4b) | effort split 45% legs, about 30% back, 25% chest; no set counts | https://fitnessvolt.com/wesley-vissers-2026-arnold-classic-training-split/ |

NOT FOUND: a coach-published per-muscle set target specific to either division (no RP, Nippard, Helms or Team Elite page was reachable that gave one).

---

## 5. What the sources say each division does NOT do

### Men's Physique

| Statement | Source |
|---|---|
| Legs have no dedicated day in the 4-5 day upper-focused split; "Day 6 is optional... light leg work"; many competitors "significantly reduce leg volume to avoid lower body overdevelopment relative to upper body"; most keep light hamstrings and calves while "reducing or eliminating heavy quad training such as squats, heavy leg press, and hack squats" | Ali Bilal, https://www.alitewear.com/blogs/learn/mens-physique-training-program |
| Legs "barely matter on stage" and "excessive leg mass can hurt the V-taper"; leg day is "maintenance volume", once a week | Ali Bilal, https://alibilalofficial.com/blog/training.html |
| Legs train at "relatively lower volume in order to focus your recovery resources towards the priority muscle groups" | Hanson, https://marketplace.trainheroic.com/workout-plan/program/hanson-program-1702922639 |
| Survey authors: "The legs are not heavily judged since athletes wear board shorts"; "calves may be used as a tiebreaker criterion"; Men's Physique athletes "may benefit from reducing chest and deltoid training to avoid overdevelopment" (an authors' suggestion, not a measured finding) | https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full |
| Hendrickson, Buendia train legs once and every other muscle once; Buendia "believes training a muscle once a week is sufficient" (Dr Workout). No fetched Men's Physique source has legs 3 or more times a week. | https://www.drworkout.fitness/jeremy-buendia-workout-routine/ ; https://jackedgorilla.com/brandon-hendrickson-workout-routine/ |
| Counter-evidence (not "does not"): Grant (off-season) and Terry (M5, M5b) train legs twice; BarBend states "Hendrickson trains legs". So "never trains legs" is NOT supported by any source fetched; "trains legs once or twice, not more" is. | https://barbend.com/news/bodybuilder-charjo-grant-week-of-training/ ; https://barbend.com/news/bodybuilder-brandon-hendrickson-v-taper-workout/ |
| Judging text: assessment excludes "the upper legs, which are covered by board shorts"; "extreme muscularity and definition should be marked down" | IFBB, https://ifbb.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Mens-Physique-2017.pdf ; NPC, https://npcnewsonline.com/official-npc-mens-physique-division/ |

### Classic Physique

| Statement | Source |
|---|---|
| No fetched Classic source trains legs fewer than once a week; the one once-weekly case is Dino (prep season) | https://generationiron.com/ramon-dino-workout/ |
| Legs are not "maintenance": Vissers allocates 45% of weekly effort to legs and says legs get "more volume, more sets, more intensity"; Ruffin trains legs three times a week in off-season; Bumstead splits quad and hamstring days | https://fitnessvolt.com/wesley-vissers-2026-arnold-classic-training-split/ ; https://barbend.com/news/terrence-ruffin-new-york-pro-physique-adjustments/ ; https://sporthiatus.com/chris-bumstead-workout-routine-complete-guide/ |
| Shoulders get comparatively less frequency in Bumstead's reconstructed cycles (1 per 8-9 days against 2 for chest, back and arms) in all three reconstructions | https://fitnessvolt.com/chris-bumstead-classic-physique-training/ ; https://sporthiatus.com/chris-bumstead-workout-routine-complete-guide/ ; https://kylehuntfitness.com/chris-bumstead-workout-and-diet-program-for-2024/ |
| Survey authors: "For Classic Physique athletes, reduced deltoid volume helps preserve proportionality" (a conclusion/suggestion; Classic off-season deltoid median 26 sets against 33.5 in Men's Physique) | https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/ |
| Survey: "Classic Bodybuilding athletes tend to perform fewer chest sets than Men's Physique athletes during the off-season" (ranks the divisions; does not mean Classic skips chest) | https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/ |
| Posing/judging: Classic has no "Most Muscular" pose; trunks are not board shorts, so legs are fully visible | https://npcnewsonline.com/classic-physique/ ; https://www.2bpevents.com/division-rules/ |

---

## 6. Gaps and cautions for the plan builder

- No source in either division is a controlled off-season template with weekly sets per muscle stated by the coach; the survey (section 4.1) is the only multi-athlete quantity.
- 3-day and 4-day structures: NOT FOUND for Classic; 3-day NOT FOUND for Men's Physique; 4-day Men's Physique rests on a single coach programme (Hanson).
- Several pages disagree with themselves (Dr Muscle for Buendia: 5 or 6 days; Fitness Volt for Cook: "6 days" for a 3-day rotation; Fitness Volt Bumstead prose against table). Day-level tables were preferred over prose.
- Dates: several pages are 2020 to 2023; Hendrickson, Buendia and Terry day-lists come from the Jacked Gorilla and Dr Workout aggregators, and Muscle and Strength states (via search only, not fetched) that its Hendrickson routine combines separate videos, so treat M1 as indicative of structure, not a current plan.
- ED-safety note: nothing in this file touches calorie floors or ED gating; it is training-structure evidence only.


---

# 07 Division research: Bikini and Wellness (off-season growth programming)

Research only. Date fetched: 2026-10-10. Every quote below was read from the page at the URL given, in this session. Where a page could not be read, the item says NOT FOUND or "search snippet only". Nothing is filled from memory. Tool note: the fetch tool summarises pages, so quotes are as returned by it; the Australian PDF was read locally with pdftotext.

Evidence grade used below: A = federation rulebook page; B = named coach or competitor, full split visible; C = commercial or unnamed page, split visible; D = survey data; E = guidance prose, no split.

---

## 1. Judging criteria

### 1.1 Bikini

| Body | Quote (verbatim) | URL |
|---|---|---|
| NPC / IFBB Pro League (NPC News Online) | Should display: "A foundation of muscle which gives shape to the female body"; "Full round glutes with a slight separation between the hamstring and glute area"; "Small amount of roundness in the delts"; "Conditioned Core" | https://npcnewsonline.com/bikini-rules/ |
| NPC (same page) | Should NOT display: "Muscular density seen in a figure physique"; "Squared glutes"; "Muscle separation seen in figure competitors"; "Graininess"; "Striations anywhere" | https://npcnewsonline.com/bikini-rules/ |
| NPC (same page) | "Muscularity – the amount of muscle will vary between the divisions"; "Condition- will vary depending on the division" | https://npcnewsonline.com/bikini-rules/ |
| OCB (second federation) | "presenting a lightly muscled and not overly lean or hard appearing physique" | https://ocbonline.com/bikini/ |
| OCB | "Bikini competitors should not display deep muscle separation, muscle striations or an abundance of muscle size." | https://ocbonline.com/bikini/ |
| OCB | "some fullness and roundness in the deltoids"; "They should show some shoulder development and back muscularity without excessive density."; "The back should show development without being overly dense or wide." | https://ocbonline.com/bikini/ |
| OCB | "some development with some fullness in the quadriceps and hamstrings"; "Legs should have muscle in the glutes, quads, hamstrings and calves, but should not be overly developed or muscular."; "full, round, firm glutes" | https://ocbonline.com/bikini/ |
| OCB | "Excessive muscularity, striations and vascularity will be marked down." | https://ocbonline.com/bikini/ |
| WFF UK (third, softer) | "The WFF Bikini athlete is softer in the body and voluptuous with a rounder shape."; "Athletes will be recognizably fit & show evidence of training without being excessively defined or dieted." | https://wff-uk.com/class-criteria/bikini/ |

Observed gaps: the NPC bikini page has no sentence about back, arms, chest or quads; it says nothing on them either way. The only upper-body sentence is the delt roundness line. UKBFF, PCA and WNBF bikini criteria: NOT FOUND (WNBF UK page returned 403; a search for UKBFF returned only costume rules).

Reading (inference, labelled): NPC rewards glutes and a little delt; penalises density, separation, striations. OCB is the only fetched body that explicitly asks for some back and quad.

### 1.2 Wellness

| Body | Quote (verbatim) | URL |
|---|---|---|
| NPC / IFBB Pro League | "The upper body is developed but not to the same degree as the lower body." | https://npcnewsonline.com/npc-and-ifbb-professional-league-wellness-rules/537583/ |
| NPC | "We are looking for conditioning almost on par with Bikini athletes, but the athletes will have slightly more muscle." | same |
| NPC | "Full round glutes with a slight separation between the hamstring and glute area but no striations,"; "Glutes will be bigger than current Bikini competitors." | same |
| NPC | Shoulders: "Slightly more developed than Bikini and a little less than Figure, but not as lean and with no striations." | same |
| NPC | Quads: "The quads should display an athletic appearance with slight separation but no striations." | same |
| NPC | "We want to see a little body fat with slight separation but no striations." | same |
| NPC (2BP events page, quoting the division) | "This division is for females with athletic physique's that showcase more body mass in the hips, glutes and thigh areas." | https://www.2bpevents.com/division-rules/ |
| IFBB Pro League Australia (PDF, read via pdftotext) | Key criteria: "Imbalance between upper and lower body- disproportionate lower body to upper body"; "Well-developed/dominant lower body muscle groups- hamstrings, quadriceps and glutes – dominant glutes over thick/dense legs." | https://www.ifbbproleague.com.au/uploads/b/994ea660-d8aa-11ed-9014-378d597f6cc4/Judging%20Criteria%20-%20Wellness.pdf |
| IFBB Pro League Australia | Front pose: "Capped shoulders slightly bigger than bikini, not overly dense nor striated."; "Hourglass shape in the upper body down through the glutes."; "Quads with slight sweep and slight separation." | same |
| IFBB Pro League Australia | Side pose: "Balanced look to the side leg-not overly quad dominant." Back pose: "Some quad sweep but not excessive." | same |
| IFBB Pro League Australia | Don't reward: "Heavily muscled and dense legs resembling Physique/BB."; "Dense/overly muscular upper body- should be more on par with bikini/slightly bigger."; "Wide/thick/dense/blocky waist."; "Overly etched in or developed hamstrings."; "Graininess- for example, veins across stomach/striations" | same |
| ANBF | "Wellness competitors should show moderate muscularity with a clear emphasis on lower-body development."; "The upper body should be developed, but it should not match the level of development in the lower body." | https://anbfnatural.com/wellness-guidelines/ |
| ANBF | "The glutes, thighs, hips, hamstrings, quads, and calves should be more developed than in Bikini." | same |
| IFBB WA | "Vertical proportions – legs to upper body length- are one the key factors."; "Physiques that are considered too muscular, too hard or too dry must be marked down." | https://www.ifbbwa.com/womens-wellness/ |

Reading (inference): in Wellness the quads are judged (sweep, separation) but must not dominate the glute; hamstrings are judged but not "etched"; upper body is explicitly "developed", with shoulders a step above Bikini. Chest and arms are not mentioned in any fetched Wellness criteria. PCA and WNBF wellness: NOT FOUND.

---

## 2. Published off-season programming

"Lift days" counts resistance sessions only. G = glute exposures, L = days with any lower-body work.

### 2.1 Bikini sources

| # | Source (grade) | URL | Lift days | Split | Glutes / lower / upper per week | Chest | Arms | Sets stated |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Breena Martinez, IFBB Bikini Pro, Iron Man magazine (B) | https://www.ironmanmagazine.com/build-a-balanced-bikini-body-with-ifbb-pro-breena-martinez/ | 5 (+1 cardio, 1 rest) | D1 Quads, glutes, abs; D2 Chest, back, abs; D3 Cardio only; D4 Hamstrings, glutes, abs; D5 Shoulders, biceps, triceps, abs; D6 Glutes, calves; D7 rest | Glutes 3 (D1, D4, D6); lower 3; upper 2. "I have seen the most results when I train glutes three days a week." | Direct, 1x | Direct, 1x (D5) | Fetch tally of listed sets: quads ~25, glutes ~26, hams 15, chest 6, back 13, shoulders 12, biceps 3, triceps 4 (tally by the fetch tool, not stated by author) |
| B2 | "Sarah, Prep Coach" (@topknotstrong), TopKnot Strong blog (C) | https://topknotstrong.com/blogs/bikini-competitors/how-to-structure-your-workouts-as-a-bikini-competitor | 5 | D1 Glutes + hamstrings; D2 Shoulders + upper body; D3 Glutes + quads; D4 Back + upper body; D5 Full-body circuit + core | Glutes 2 in sample ("2-3 times per week" in text); lower 2; upper 2-3 ("shoulders and back ... 1-2 times per week") | Not mentioned | Not mentioned | None |

Lower 2 plus glutes on D3 (glute-involving days 3); upper 3 | One exercise on D2 "if no implants, or implants not under the muscle"; also in D6 full upper | Triceps D2, biceps D3, possibly D6 | None. | B4 | Coach Mark Carroll (C) | https://coachmarkcarroll.com/learn-post/best-bikini-body-workout-plan/ | 4 | Mon Lower 1 (glutes, hams, quads); Tue Upper 1 (shoulders, back, chest, abs); Thu Lower 2; Fri Upper 2 | Each muscle twice; glutes 2, lower 2, upper 2 | Push-ups only in example | None in either example workout | None |
| B5 | Move With Us "Bikini Build" programme, 4/5/6-day options (C, unnamed author) | https://support.movewithus.com/en/articles/10123605-what-is-the-training-split-for-bikini-build-program | 3-5 weighted per level | 6-day, Level 1: Lower (glutes, hams); Upper (back, shoulders, chest); HIIT+core; Lower (quads, hams); Upper (shoulders, back, chest); Glutes | Level 1: 3 lower, 2 upper; Level 3: 3 lower (incl. a glutes day), 2 upper (+ full body) | Yes in Levels 1, 3, 4; not Level 2 | Mostly no; Level 3 only (biceps once, triceps once) | None |
| B6 | Mikolo gym blog (C, no author) | https://gym-mikolo.com/blogs/home-gym/the-ultimate-bikini-competitor-workout-plan-training-for-success | 4 (+ core day) | D1 Upper (shoulders, chest, triceps); D2 Lower (glutes, hams, quads); D4 Core; D5 Upper (back, biceps); D6 Lower | Glutes 2, lower 2, upper 2 | Direct, 1x | Triceps D1, biceps D5 | None |
| B7 | Julie Lohre, IFBB Fitness Pro, Beverly International (undated) (B) | https://beverlyinternational.com/posted_articles/total-bikini-competitors-program/ | 3 | D1 Shoulders, biceps, triceps; D3 Quads, glutes, hams, calves; D5 Back, chest, abs; cardio on D2, D4, D6 | Each muscle once per week; glutes 1 | Direct, 1x | Direct, 1x | Circuits of 2-3 rounds only |

Guidance-only bikini sources (no split), all fetched:
- Darin Starr, Five Starr Physique. Chest: "no competitor has ever been told to work on their pecs." Arms: "Keep arm work minimal"; "If you have a dedicated arm day, you're overtraining them." Upper body "less than 50%" of total volume for most bikini and wellness competitors. https://fivestarrphysique.com/bodybuilding-contest-prep/upper-body-balance-for-bikini/
- Renaissance Periodization Strength podcast page. "Bikini division emphasizes glutes, shoulders, and overall shape-not quad mass." For a shoulder-dominant athlete, cut direct delt work to "one session weekly" and raise lat rowing to three sessions. No weekly split or set totals. https://rpstrength.com/blogs/podcasts/the-science-of-training-female-bodybuilders-division-specific-strategies
- Bret Contreras. His programmes "have you hitting the glutes 3-6 days per week" versus "Most programs have you hitting the glutes 1-2 days per week"; do not give the upper body "the same effort you hammer the glutes". https://bretcontreras.com/how-to-train-for-a-bikini-competition-its-all-about-glutes/

Paul Revelia: only a search snippet ("five training days per week, with glutes and shoulders as the emphasis", Bodcast with Chloe Madeley, https://music.amazon.com/es-cl/podcasts/08ab0b0f-7b92-44e5-95b2-bec89fbd43ed/episodes/5386eca6-2e46-4e1b-aba2-a7029b0e4192/the-bodcast-with-chloe-madeley-paul-revelia-on-bikini-coaching-programming-reverse-dieting-into-shows-and-more). The page itself did not render, so this is NOT verified and not counted. Glam Girl Bikini / Natalie Matthews, Lauren Findley (Playbook 403), Cliff Wilson, Eric Helms wellness/bikini split, Team Elite Physique: NOT FOUND as a readable split. Team Dee's page (https://teamdees.com/ultimate-exercise-regimens-for-bikini-bodybuilding/) gave no split, only "Bicep Curls, & Tricep Dips To Build Symmetry & Aesthetics".

Bikini count: 7 sources with a visible split (3 by named coach or pro: B1, B3, B7; B2 is a named prep coach by first name only), plus 3 guidance pages. Four or more distinct sources: met.

Guidance-only wellness sources:
- RP Strength page (above): "Wellness requires dramatically more glute emphasis than bikini"; higher volume hip-dominant work and accepts more quad development.
- Darin Starr (above): upper body "less than 50%" of total volume for bikini and wellness.
- BarBend Mattos page: lists lower-body exercises only (sumo squats on blocks, stiff-leg deadlifts, Bulgarian split squats, barbell glute bridges, side leg lift, cable glute kickback); no frequency. https://barbend.com/francielle-mattos/
- Isabelle Nunes, Angelica Teixeira's coach, Eric Helms, Cliff Wilson on wellness: NOT FOUND. Lipstick Lifters and MyCompPrep pages returned no body text (fetch empty). A Scribd Isa Pereira programme failed to load.

Wellness count: 4 sources with a visible split (W1-W4), 2 further (W5, W6) with partial structure, 2 guidance pages. Four or more distinct sources: met, but W4 is anonymous.

---

## 3. Modal structure by training days

Only sources with a visible split are counted. Samples are small, so "modal" here means the most common pattern in a handful of pages, not a statistical mode.

### 3.1 Bikini

| Days | Modal session list (in order) | Exposures per week | Sources for / against |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Shoulders + arms; Quads/glutes/hams/calves; Back + chest + abs | Every muscle once; glutes 1 | B7 only. No other 3-day page found. Thin evidence. |
| 4 | Lower (glutes, hams, quads); Upper (shoulders, back, chest); Lower 2; Upper 2 | Glutes 2, lower 2, upper 2, chest 1-2, arms 0-1 | B4 and B6 agree (alternating lower/upper). B5 has a 4-day option but its pattern is unspecified. 2 of 2 clear. |
| 5 | Two lower sessions (one glute/ham, one glute/quad) + two to three upper sessions (shoulders-led; back-led; optional full upper) | Glutes 2-3, lower 2-3, upper 2-3 | B2: 2 lower, 2-3 upper, glutes 2. B3: 2 lower plus glute-containing D3, 3 upper, glutes 3. B1: 3 lower, 2 upper, glutes 3. Glutes 3x in 2 of 3, 2x in 1 of 3. Lower sessions 2 in 2 of 3, 3 in 1 of 3. |
| 6 | 3 lower sessions (glute/ham, quad/glute, glute-only) + 2 upper (shoulders, back, chest) + cardio/core | Glutes 3, lower 3, upper 2 | B5 (Level 1, 6-day: 3 lower, 2 upper, 1 HIIT+core), B1 (5 lifts, 1 cardio). 2 sources, both 3 lower. B3 is a 7-day layout with 5 lifts. |

Arms and chest in the seven bikini sources with a split: chest is trained directly or semi-directly in six (B1, B3 as one optional exercise, B4 as push-ups only, B5 in three of four levels, B6, B7); B2 is silent. Direct arm work appears in four (B1, B3, B6, B7); B4 and B5 Level 2 have none; B2 is silent. Sources therefore disagree on arms (4 yes, 2 no, 1 silent); Starr and Lenihan ask for restraint. (Em dash in one verbatim RP quote was replaced by a hyphen.)

### 3.2 Wellness

| Days | Modal session list | Exposures per week | Sources for / against |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | NOT FOUND | | No wellness page with a 3-day split fetched. |
| 4 | Lower (glute/quad); Upper; Glutes + hamstrings; Upper/shoulders. Variant: Glutes; Quads; Upper maintenance; Hams + glutes | Glutes 2, lower 2-3, upper 1-2 | W3 is 2 lower + 2 upper; W1 (4-day reading) is 3 lower + 1 upper. 1 each way. |
| 5 | Glutes (or glute/ham); Quads; Upper maintenance (shoulders/back/arms); Hams + glutes; 2nd upper | Glutes 2, lower 3, upper 2 | W1 5-day version and W4 both 3 lower + 2 upper. 2 of 2. |
| 6 | Lower; Upper; Quad focus; Upper; Glute/ham focus; Quad focus | Lower 4, upper 2; quads 2-3, glutes/hams 2 | W2 only (4 lower + 2 upper). W5 (7 days) has 3 heavy lower days. W6 says lower 5x per week. All point to lower-heavy; only one gives a full 6-day list. |

Chest in wellness sources with a split: direct in 1 of 4 (W1, light); none in W2, W3, W4. Arms direct in 4 of 4 (W1, W2, W3, W4), at roughly one or two sessions a week.

### 3.3 Differences, plainly stated (evidence, then inference)

- Observed: Bikini splits contain 2 to 3 upper sessions with shoulder and back work in nearly every case; Wellness splits contain 1 to 2 upper sessions, with 3 to 4 lower sessions at 5 to 6 days.
- Observed: only Wellness sources separate quads onto their own day (W1, W2, W4); Bikini sources rarely do (B1 does, quads on D1).
- Inference: the number of lower-body sessions is the main structural difference between the two divisions in this sample.

---

## 4. Per-muscle weekly set ranges

### 4.1 Survey of competing athletes (best numeric source found)

Source: Frontiers in Sports and Active Living 2025, "Quantification of weekly strength-training volume per muscle group in competitive physique athletes", read as PMC12345604, Tables 5 and 7. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/ (also https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full, listed in search, not fetched). Values are median weekly sets (min to max). Bikini n = 9, Wellness n = 20, all female. Participants had competed within two years; the page does not state country (ethics body in Sao Paulo; federations listed were largely Brazilian). Self-reported; the page reports no significant off-season versus pre-contest difference for these two categories.

| Muscle | Bikini off-season | Wellness off-season | Direct training? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chest | 7.5 (0 to 24) | 1.0 (0 to 16) | Bikini median 7.5; Wellness median 1, so most Wellness athletes do almost none |
| Biceps | 7.5 (0 to 18) | 8.0 (0 to 24) | Yes in both |
| Triceps | 7.5 (0 to 18) | 10.0 (0 to 26) | Yes in both |
| Quadriceps | 7.5 (1 to 20) | 28.0 (1 to 60) | Bikini low; Wellness high |
| Glutes | 31.0 (2 to 41) | 24.0 (3 to 90) | Yes in both |
| Hamstrings | 17.0 (2 to 32) | 24.0 (1 to 40) | Yes in both |
| Deltoids | 7.5 (2 to 48) | 18.0 (0 to 50) | Bikini median low, range wide |
| Lats | 12.0 (1 to 25) | 16.0 (0 to 32) | Yes |
| Calves | 8.0 (3 to 24) | 12.0 (0 to 36) | Yes |

Caveats: Bikini n = 9 is very small; the ranges are wide enough that the medians describe the middle athlete, not a rule.

### 4.2 Individual published programmes (from section 2)

| Muscle | Figure | Source URL |
|---|---|---|
| Bikini glutes | ~26 listed sets (fetch tally) | https://www.ironmanmagazine.com/build-a-balanced-bikini-body-with-ifbb-pro-breena-martinez/ |
| Bikini quads | ~25 listed sets (fetch tally) | same |
| Bikini hamstrings | 15 | same |
| Bikini chest | 6 | same |
| Bikini biceps / triceps | 3 / 4 | same |
| Bikini shoulders / back | 12 / 13 | same |
| Bikini glute day content | "just 2-3 glute exercises...no more!" on the glutes/back/biceps day | https://nextlevelbikiniprep.com/5-keys-bikini-competition-training/ |
| Bikini/Wellness upper body share | "less than 50%" of total volume | https://fivestarrphysique.com/bodybuilding-contest-prep/upper-body-balance-for-bikini/ |
| Wellness upper-body day | "two to three sets per movement" | https://ryanvltn.com/blog/wellness-division-bodybuilding |
| Per exercise, generic | "3-4 exercises per muscle group" per session, "2-3 working sets per exercise" (per session, not weekly) | https://rpstrength.com/blogs/podcasts/the-science-of-training-female-bodybuilders-division-specific-strategies |
| Direct sets per muscle in sources B2-B7, W1-W6 | NOT FOUND (no set counts given) | |

Joe Bennett's "12 to 20 hard weekly sets" appeared only in a search summary of a BarBend article, not fetched, so it is not used.

### 4.3 Do the sources train the de-emphasised muscles directly?

| Muscle | Bikini | Wellness |
|---|---|---|
| Chest | Mostly yes, small: survey median 7.5 sets; B1 6 sets; Lenihan one optional exercise; Starr says chest not a priority | Mostly negligible: survey median 1 set; only W1 trains it, light and high rep |
| Biceps | Yes, low: survey 7.5; B1 3 sets | Yes: survey 8; W1-W4 all include |
| Triceps | Yes, low: survey 7.5; B1 4 sets | Yes: survey 10; W1-W4 all include |
| Quads | Low: survey 7.5 (range 1 to 20); B1 ~25 is higher; RP says not quad mass | High: survey 28; W1, W2, W4 give quads a day |

Disagreement within Bikini: the survey median for quads (7.5) is far below the one published pro split (B1, ~25 sets). One pro's routine, one n = 9 survey: not a resolved answer.

---

## 5. What the sources say the division does NOT do

### Bikini
- No heavy quad building: "Bikini division emphasizes glutes, shoulders, and overall shape-not quad mass." https://rpstrength.com/blogs/podcasts/the-science-of-training-female-bodybuilders-division-specific-strategies
- Not much upper back: "Most bikini girls shouldn't do TOO much upper back work (judges don't want much development there)." https://nextlevelbikiniprep.com/5-keys-bikini-competition-training/
- Not much adduction, oblique or direct ab work before lean: "You really can't afford to do a lot of hip ADDuction"; "Avoid side bends/side planks, and torso rotation." Same URL.
- No dedicated arm day: "If you have a dedicated arm day, you're overtraining them." https://fivestarrphysique.com/bodybuilding-contest-prep/upper-body-balance-for-bikini/
- No pec focus: "no competitor has ever been told to work on their pecs." Same URL.
- Not striated, dense or separated: NPC "Should NOT display" list (section 1.1), https://npcnewsonline.com/bikini-rules/
- Not equal-effort upper body: Contreras advises against giving upper body muscles "the same effort you hammer the glutes". https://bretcontreras.com/how-to-train-for-a-bikini-competition-its-all-about-glutes/
- Not a counter-claim: OCB asks for "some shoulder development and back muscularity" and "some development with some fullness in the quadriceps", so a fully minimal quad or back approach is not universal. https://ocbonline.com/bikini/

### Wellness
- No big shoulders or wide back: "Judges do NOT want big shoulders or a wide back."; "Do NOT flare your lats. That's a figure cue, not a wellness cue."; "Wellness is not a shredded division." https://ryanvltn.com/blog/wellness-division-bodybuilding
- No dense upper body: "Dense/overly muscular upper body- should be more on par with bikini/slightly bigger." https://www.ifbbproleague.com.au/uploads/b/994ea660-d8aa-11ed-9014-378d597f6cc4/Judging%20Criteria%20-%20Wellness.pdf
- No quad-dominant legs or etched hamstrings: "Balanced look to the side leg-not overly quad dominant."; "Overly etched in or developed hamstrings." Same URL.
- No heavy chest work in most programmes: survey median 1 set (section 4.1); chest absent from W2, W3, W4.
- Stay under the division line: "it's essential to avoid excessive muscle mass that could push you into different divisions." https://www.roxstarfitness.com/the-ultimate-beginners-guide-to-wellness-physique-competitions/
- No striations: NPC "no striations" in glutes, shoulders and quads. https://npcnewsonline.com/npc-and-ifbb-professional-league-wellness-rules/537583/

---

## 6. Limits of this research

- Sample sizes are small and mixed in quality; only B1, B3, B7, W1, W2, W3 are named people with a full visible split. Five or six of the 13 splits are blog or commercial pages.
- No fetched source gave weekly direct-set targets per muscle for a recommended plan; the only set figures are the 2025 survey medians and one athlete's listed sets.
- UKBFF, PCA, WNBF criteria, Revelia, Findley, Helms, Nunes, Teixeira and Team Elite Physique splits: NOT FOUND as readable pages this session.
- Do not treat the "modal" rows as evidence-weighted: they are counts of pages (section 3).


---

# Division research: Figure and Women's Physique (off-season programming)

Date fetched: 2026-10-10. Method: every statement below was read on a page fetched this session; URL inline. Anything not found says NOT FOUND. Modal structures in section 3 are my own tally of the fetched splits (labelled as such), not a quoted claim.

## 0. Evidence quality, read first

- Figure: 6 sources with a day-level split were fetched (section 2A). Only 2 are off-season-labelled-as-such in any way; none states sets per muscle per week for the whole week except the Beverly programme (countable from its exercise list).
- Women's Physique (WP): only 4 day-level splits were fetched, and NONE is a published off-season programme by a named NPC/IFBB Pro WP competitor on her own channel. One is a third-party page on Dana Linn Bailey (WP Olympia champion); three are TrainHeroic coach templates whose division is not stated as NPC WP (two are tagged "Bodybuilding").
- Not fetchable this session: npcnewsonline.com rules pages (HTTP 307, not followed), elitefts "8-Week Off-Season Figure Training Plan" (HTTP 404, and web.archive.org connection reset), Erin Stern shoulder article on fitnessvolt (partly read), barbend Cydney Gillon leg article (HTTP 410), NASM blog (redirected to a blog index). Their content is NOT used.
- Searched, nothing usable found: Sarah Villegas, Cliff Wilson, Joe Bennett, Paul Revelia, 3DMJ, Team Elite Physique (as a coach brand) for either division; Natalia Coelho day-level split; Renaissance Periodization day-by-day split (only structure).
- PCA, UKBFF, IFBB amateur judging text: NOT FOUND (no page fetched).

## 1. Judging criteria

### 1A. Figure

| Body | Quoted criteria | URL |
|---|---|---|
| NPC Worldwide rules (hosted on IFBB Pro) | "A small degree of muscularity with separation but no visible striations"; "Toned muscle with shapely lines and overall firmness but not excessively lean"; "Overall healthy appearance, including skin tone and makeup"; "Overall stage presentation". No muscle group is named on that page. | https://www.ifbbpro.com/npc-worldwide/rules/ |
| OCB | "Judging for Figure is based on symmetry, muscularity and conditioning." "Leanness and muscle development are expected in figure, but NOT to the same degree as presented in Women's Physique." "Balanced muscle proportion from top to bottom." "Looking for an 'X' frame with capped shoulders, wide lats, small waist, full round, glutes, sweeping quads". "Lean, visible separation/cuts in quads, hamstrings, abdominals, and back." "Not grainy or overly striated muscles, with the possible exception of the shoulders". Marked down: "deeply drawn-in faces or stringy-looking muscularity (an emaciated look)"; "less shapely muscularity due to muscle glycogen depletion". | https://ocbonline.com/figure/ |
| WNBF (2025 judging deck, PDF) | Two rounds: Symmetry, Muscle Tone. "Figure competitors should be more muscular than a bikini competitor". "Bodybuilding muscularity does not fit this category and is scored down". | https://wnbf.no/documents/2025%20Updated%20Official%20WNBF%20Judging%20Rules%20EDITED.pdf (slide text extracted with pdftotext) |
| Coach explainer (Heather Owen, not a federation) | "Shoulders, back, quads and glutes are very important muscle groups to give the "x" type of shape." Judges want separation but not striations. | https://getfitgofigure.com/divisions-explained-womens-bikini-figure-physique-bodybuilding/ |

### 1B. Women's Physique

| Body | Quoted criteria | URL |
|---|---|---|
| NPC Worldwide rules (on IFBB Pro) | "Competitors should display a toned, athletic physique showcasing femininity, muscle tone, and the beauty and flow of the physique." "Women's Physique competitors should have the overall aesthetics and look that is found in Figure ... with a little more overall muscularity." "Judges shall score competitors according to the 'total package', which is a balance of size, symmetry, and muscularity." "Excessive muscularity should be scored down." Terms that should not describe WP: "ripped, shredded, peeled, striated, dry, diced, hard, vascular, grainy, massive, thick, dense, etc." No muscle group is named. | https://www.ifbbpro.com/npc-worldwide/rules/ |
| OCB | "The upper and lower body should be balanced and proportioned in terms of muscularity and conditioning." "Women's physique should display the most muscularity of the four categories." Marked down: "deeply drawn-in faces, a stringy-looking muscularity (an emaciated look)", "less shapely muscularity due to muscle glycogen depletion". NOTE: this conflicts in direction with NPC "excessive muscularity should be scored down"; OCB sets the bar higher on size. | https://ocbonline.com/womens-physique/ |
| WNBF | Women's Physique: NOT FOUND (the deck's slides list Figure, Bikini, Fit Body, Bodybuilding, Classic, Men's Physique only). | same PDF as above |
| Coach explainer (Owen) | "Visible muscle separation and some visible striations are desired but are not to be excessively muscular or shredded." (This differs from the NPC term list, which bans "striated".) | https://getfitgofigure.com/divisions-explained-womens-bikini-figure-physique-bodybuilding/ |

Abs Mon/Wed/Fri. | Legs 2, back 2, delts 2, biceps 2, triceps 2, chest 1, abs 3 |

Frontiers 2025 (n=154 survey; Figure n=3, WP n=3, so figures are indicative only): https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full . Figure and WP figures in section 4. Whole-sample statement: "In off-season most physique athletes use split routines (85.5%), train 4-7 times per week, target major muscle groups twice weekly, and sessions last 60-90 min. Typical sessions involve 2-3 muscle groups, 2-3 exercises per group, 3-4 sets per exercise, 7-12 repetitions per set". Same paper on glutes: "The Wellness category requires larger buttocks than Bikini and Figure, and often also more voluminous than Women's Physique".

Searched but NOT usable: Elite FTS Dave Tate 8-week off-season figure plan (https://www.elitefts.com/education/8-week-off-season-figure-training-plan/ returned 404 on fetch; I only saw a search summary, so nothing is taken from it).

## 3. Modal structure by training days (my tally of the fetched splits)

Counts are of splits, not of independent athletes. Where a count is 1 or 2 the "mode" is only a description of what exists.

### 3A. Figure

| Days | Session list in order | Exposures/week | Support and disagreement |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Chest + Back; Delts; Legs | each 1 | 1 source (F2). Nothing else at 3 days. No arms or abs. |
| 4 | Shoulders/Back; Legs/Glutes; Chest/Arms; Legs/Glutes | legs 2; shoulders, back, chest, arms 1 | F4 only has legs twice. Owen beginner 4-day (Chest+Tri; Legs; Back+Bi; Shoulders) has legs 1. Split 1 v 1. |
| 5 | Legs; Back/Delts/Biceps; Chest/Triceps; Legs (glutes); Delts/Back/Biceps/Triceps | legs 2, back 2, delts 2, chest 1, arms 2 | F6 only. F1 is 5 weight days but legs 3, upper once each (fat-loss, not off-season). |
| 6 | Legs; Chest; Back; Legs; Shoulders; Arms (F3) | legs 2, rest 1 | 3 six-day splits: F3 (legs 2, others 1); Owen prog 1 (every muscle 2, pairs); Owen prog 2 (legs 1, shoulders 2, rest 1). 3 different answers. |

Across all 8 Figure splits (F1, F2, F3, F4, F5 x3, F6): legs 2 sessions in 4, 1 session in 3, 3 sessions in 1. Shoulders (including combined days) 1 in 5, 2 in 3. Chest 1 in 7, 2 in 1. Chest is trained on its own session or paired in all 8 splits; arms are trained directly in 7 of 8 (F2 lists none). Sessions combining shoulders with back appear in F4, F5 prog 1, F6; sessions pairing chest with arms or triceps in F1, F4, F5, F6.

### 3B. Women's Physique

| Days | Session list in order | Exposures/week | Support and disagreement |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Lower days 1-3 option of W2 (Glutes+Ham; Chest+Lats; Squat lower) | lower 2, upper 1 | 1 source, and it is the first three days of a 5-day template. |
| 4 | Lower; Upper; Lower; Upper | glutes/quads/hams 2; upper muscles 1-2 | W3 and W4 agree (2 of 2). Frontiers: whole sample 4-7 days, "major muscle groups twice weekly". |
| 5 | W1: Chest; Shoulders; Back; Legs; Arms. W2: Glutes+Ham; Chest+Lats; Squat lower; Shoulders+upper back; Glutes+Arms | W1 all 1; W2 lower 3, upper 1 | 1 v 1, opposite shapes (body part v lower-heavy). W2 is not confirmed as NPC WP. |
| 6 | NOT FOUND for WP | | |

Legs/lower sessions per week across WP splits W1-W4: 1, 3, 2, 2. Shoulder-specific sessions: W1 1, W2 1 (with upper back), W3 and W4 unspecified (inside upper days). Chest direct: W1 yes, W2 yes, W3 labelled "High Incline Press" yes, W4 unclear. Arms direct: W1 yes, W2 yes (Glutes and arms), W3 yes (curl, pushdown, skullcrusher), W4 unclear.

## 4. Direct sets per muscle per week

### 4A. Frontiers 2025, off-season median (min-max), pre-contest median in brackets. n = 3 per division. https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full

| Muscle | Figure | Women's Physique |
|---|---|---|
| Pectoral | 12 (8-16) [16] | 2 (0-4) [4] |
| Latissimus dorsi | 12 (0-24) [24] | 17 (4-30) [30] |
| Deltoid | 23 (16-30) [30] | 20 (4-36) [36] |
| Biceps | 12.5 (12-13) [13] | 11 (4-18) [18] |
| Triceps | 12.5 (12-12) [12] | 11 (4-18) [18] |
| Quadriceps | 28.5 (23-34) [34] | 17 (4-30) [30] |
| Hamstrings | 27 (14-40) [40] | 11 (4-18) [24] |
| Gluteal | 13.5 (13-14) [14] | 11 (4-18) [18] |
| Triceps surae (calves) | 17.5 (17-18) [17] | 9.5 (5-14) [14] |
| Abdominals | 12 (0-24) [12] | 2 (0-4) [8] |
| Lower back | 1.5 (0-3) [3] | 2 (0-4) [4] |

Read: with n=3, WP chest 2 and abs 2 sets mean a tiny sample, not a division norm. Figure deltoids (23) were the highest upper-body figure in that sample; quads 28.5 and hamstrings 27 the highest lower. Frontiers also gives Bikini and Wellness for comparison (same URL).

| | Abs | 2 moves x 3 sets on 5 days (30) | Yes |
| Owen (F5 prog 2) https://getfitgofigure.com/10-training-tips-for-the-figure-competitor/ | Per session | 3-5 exercises x 3-4 sets, 8-12 reps | Yes |
| DLB via TotalShape (W1) https://totalshape.com/fitness/dana-linn-bailey-workout-routine-and-diet-plan/ | Chest | about 21-23 in one session | Yes |
| | Shoulders | 28 | Yes |
| | Back | 32 | Yes |
| | Legs | 29 | Yes |
| | Arms | 32 | Yes |
| | Abs, calves, glutes | not listed | No |
| Erin Stern glute session https://barbend.com/news/program-glutes-erin-stern/ | Glutes | 14-20 sets in one session across 6 exercises; page does not give weekly frequency | Yes |
| Erin Stern shoulder session https://fitnessvolt.com/2x-figure-olympia-erin-stern-shares-how-to-grow-shoulder-width-w-latest-workout/ | Shoulders | one session of 6 movements: press 3-5, laterals 3-4, face pull 3-4, band pull-apart 2-3, two others unspecified; weekly frequency not stated | Yes |

Coach generic text on chest, quads and abs in WP: NOT FOUND beyond W1-W3 above.

## 5. What the sources say the division does NOT do

- Figure: no visible striations; not excessively lean (https://www.ifbbpro.com/npc-worldwide/rules/). Bodybuilding muscularity "is scored down" (WNBF PDF above). No deeply drawn-in faces or stringy muscle (https://ocbonline.com/figure/). Gillon trains biceps with lower volume than other upper-body muscles because they "grow very readily" (https://www.kaged.com/blogs/cydney-gillon-figure-trainer/cgfigure-training). A very developed upper body with no leg muscle is not wanted: "You don't want a very developed upper body and no leg muscle" (https://getfitgofigure.com/10-training-tips-for-the-figure-competitor/). Figure glutes are smaller than Wellness in the Frontiers paper's description (URL in section 2A).
- Women's Physique: not described as ripped, shredded, peeled, striated, dry, diced, hard, vascular, grainy, massive, thick, dense; "Excessive muscularity should be scored down" (https://www.ifbbpro.com/npc-worldwide/rules/). OCB says the opposite on size ("the most muscularity of the four categories") but still marks down stringy, depleted muscle (https://ocbonline.com/womens-physique/).
- Training: the Frontiers WP medians show chest (2) and abs (2) near zero in that sample (n=3; not a rule). W1 lists no direct abs, calf or glute work (TotalShape URL above). No source I fetched states that either division omits a named muscle group from training; that claim is NOT FOUND.

## 6. Not found / gaps to resolve before this feeds plan logic

- NPC rules pages on npcnewsonline.com were not readable; the IFBB Pro-hosted NPC Worldwide page was used instead.
- No named WP athlete's own off-season split with a stated division and phase; W1 is third-party.
- No 6-day WP split; no 3-day Figure split other than F2.
- No sets-per-muscle statement from any coach for WP; Frontiers n=3 is the only quantification.
- PCA, UKBFF, IFBB amateur criteria: NOT FOUND.


---

<!-- source file: docs/audit/plan-builder-science-2026-10-04/09-DIVISION-RESEARCH-BODYBUILDING-GENERAL-SURVEY.md -->

# 09. Division research: survey of physique athletes, open bodybuilding, general hypertrophy

Research only, 2026-10-10. Every figure below was read from a page fetched this session; the URL is inline. "NOT FOUND" means I could not source it. Where a fetch tool summarised a page for me rather than returning raw text, or where I only saw a search-result snippet, that is flagged. Where a sample is mine (a handful of named splits), "modal" means modal within that handful, not a population statistic.

---

## PART A. The survey

Source: da Silveira et al., Frontiers in Sports and Active Living, 30 July 2025, DOI 10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360. PMC page: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12345604/ . The PMC page fetch returned only a summary, so all numbers below were read from the full PDF text (Padua repository copy of the same article): https://www.research.unipd.it/bitstream/11577/3557939/1/de%20Silveira%20(2025)%20-%20Quantification%20of%20weekly%20strength-training%20volume%20per%20muscle%20group%20in%20competitive%20physique%20athletes.pdf (Tables 1 to 7). Frontiers full text: https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/full

### A1. Design, sample, terminology

- Online cross-sectional survey (Google Forms, English and Portuguese), April to December 2020. Inclusion: affiliated to a federation, competed at least once in the last two years, training written down or on a spreadsheet.
- n = 154. Federations: NPC Pro League 85, IFBB Elite Pro 39, SPFF 19, WBFF 2, IFBB SP 1, NABBA 1, WBFF Pro 1, WFF 1, other 5.
- The paper says "pré-contest", not "in-season". I map "in-season" to pré-contest below. No other period (peak week, post-show) is reported.
- All periods are self-reported from written programmes; values are weekly sets (median, with min to max in brackets). Table 1 also gives body mass and height by category.

| Division (paper's label) | Sex | n | Table 1 body mass off-season / pré-contest (kg, mean) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Master | M | 11 | 101.56 / 89.18 |
| Bodybuilding Classic | M | 14 | 93.00 / 76.43 |
| Classic Physique | M | 26 | 95.90 / 79.36 |
| Senior | M | 18 | 103.27 / 88.76 |
| Men's Physique | M | 50 | 88.60 / 78.25 |
| Wellness | F | 20 | 66.07 / 59.95 |
| Women's Physique | F | 3 | 80.50 / 63.33 |
| Bikini | F | 9 | 62.00 / 52.56 |
| Figure | F | 3 | 60.00 / 57.00 |

Absent or unclear: there is no row labelled "men's open bodybuilding" or "women's bodybuilding". "Bodybuilding Classic", "Master" and "Senior" are the paper's labels and the paper does not define them in the text I read; I cannot say from the page whether "Bodybuilding Classic" is open or classic-style (NOT FOUND). Women's Physique n = 3 and Figure n = 3, so their "medians" are of three people (the paper's tables give "p-value = NA" for Figure and Women's Physique in Table 5).

### A2. What counts as a set (definition and counting rules)

The paper contains no sentence that defines "a set" and no direct versus fractional rule. The methods text, verbatim from the PDF:

> "The weekly training volume for each muscle group was calculated based on equations used in prior studies (17, 18)."

followed by three listed inputs:

> "Number of exercises per muscle group per training session / Number of sets per exercise in each training session / Weekly training frequency per muscle group"

The muscle-group sentence, verbatim:

> "The study focused on analyzing the most commonly trained muscle groups in hypertrophy-oriented programs: pectoralis major, latissimus dorsi, deltoids, biceps brachii, triceps brachii, gluteal muscles, quadriceps, hamstrings, gastrocnemius (triceps surae), and abdominal muscles. For each muscle group, commonly performed exercises (e.g., French press for the triceps brachii) were included in the analysis. Table 2 provides a comprehensive list of exercises considered for each muscle group. All variations of these exercises were included, regardless of the training modality utilized, such as free weights, machines, pulley systems, or bodyweight exercises."

Observed consequence: weekly sets per muscle = exercises per session x sets per exercise x weekly frequency, summed over the exercises in the paper's Table 2 list. Fractional or indirect credit is not mentioned anywhere I read (NOT FOUND). Lower back appears as an eleventh row in the result tables although it is not in the muscle-group sentence.

Table 2 (exercises counted, as extracted; the PDF layout is garbled, so row-to-muscle alignment of a few entries is my reading):

| Muscle | Exercises listed in Table 2 |
|---|---|
| Pectoral | Bench press (all variations with wide grip); fly (all variations); crossover/pec deck; pullover (placement ambiguous in the layout) |
| Latissimus dorsi | Pulldown (all variations); pull up; lat pull down; rowing (all variations with closed grip); Graviton; deadlift |
| Deltoid | Shoulder press (all variations); upright row; raise (lateral, frontal). No rear-delt exercise is listed in Table 2. |
| Biceps and triceps | Elbow curl (all variations); elbow extension (all variations) |
| Gluteal / quadriceps | Hip extension; squat; leg press; lunge; hip thrust; knee extension |
| Hamstrings | Knee curl (all variations); stiff deadlift; Nordic hamstrings |
| Abdominals | Trunk flexion (all variations) |
| Triceps surae | Ankle extension (all variations) |

Deltoids: one pooled "Deltoid" row (front, side, upright row); rear-delt work is not itemised. Back/lats: one "Latissimus dorsi" row that includes closed-grip rowing, pulldowns, pull-ups and deadlift, so it is a whole-back figure, not lats alone. Abs: one "Abdominals" row, trunk flexion only. Lower back is a separate row. Because the deadlift appears under lats and stiff deadlift under hamstrings, one movement can be counted toward more than one group; the paper does not say (NOT FOUND).

Caveats observed in the tables: the Women's Physique pectoral, biceps and triceps ranges are identical-looking for biceps and triceps (11 (4-18) / 18 (4-36) for both), which with n = 3 suggests thin data. Bikini glute drops 31 to 15 (-51.6 per cent) with a 2 to 41 range, which is wide for 9 respondents. Figure n = 3, so no test was possible. Table 8 (cardio) is not reproduced here.

### A5. Paper-level statements worth carrying

- Men higher upper-body volume, women higher lower-body volume (Discussion; abstract snippet in search result: https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/sports-and-active-living/articles/10.3389/fspor.2025.1536360/pdf ). In the PDF text: men lower-body 23.8 +/- 11.2 vs women 11.5 +/- 7.0 sets (the line I read in the discussion was cut by column layout; treat the exact pairing as unverified).
- Most categories reduced volume pré-contest for most muscles, with abdominals and cardio the exception (cardio rose; abdominals up for Bodybuilding Classic and Master).
- Self-reported, 2020, mostly Brazilian-affiliated sample (SPFF, São Paulo ethics approval), n small in four divisions. It is not a prescription.

---

## PART B. Open bodybuilding and general hypertrophy

### B1. Open bodybuilding judging criteria (quoted)

| Federation | Quote | URL |
|---|---|---|
| NPC / IFBB Pro League | "Judges will score competitors according to the 'total package', which is a balance of size, symmetry and muscularity." Page also lists mandatory poses: Front Double Biceps, Front Lat Spread, Side Chest, Back Double Biceps, Back Lat Spread, Side Triceps, Abdominals and Thighs; "maximum of 60 seconds" for mandatory poses. It does not separately define conditioning or presentation for this division. | https://npcnewsonline.com/ifbb-pro-league-rules/ |
| NPC promoter rules page (Tim Gardner Productions) | "Judges will score competitors according to the IFBB Pro League 'total package' which is balance of size, symmetry and muscularity." | https://www.timgardnerproductions.com/rules |
| NANBF (natural federation) | "Symmetry and muscularity." "A balanced proportioned physique top to bottom, front to back, side to side." "Muscle mass, shape." "Conditioning, hardness, separation." "How well the competitor executes posing to create symmetrical harmony in group comparisions." "Stage presentation, tan, suit choice, grooming, posture." | https://nanbf.net/nansite/mens-bodybuilding/ |

Not found: the IFBB Pro League's own rulebook text (the NPC news page is the NPC/IFBB Pro League qualifier rules). Weightings (e.g. 40/30/30) appeared only on low-quality sites in search results and are NOT sourced.

Implication for a plan builder (observed vs inferred): all three criteria sets reward whole-body balance ("top to bottom, front to back, side to side") and include legs and back by definition; none says "upper-body priority". The balance reading is the criteria's own wording; any programming consequence is my inference.

### B2. Published splits: open bodybuilding (pros and coaches)

Seven found. Several are third-party write-ups, not the athlete's own publication; the source's own caveat is noted. Set counts per muscle are only what the page lists.

Not obtained: Chris Aceto (search returned only a forum column advising each body part "once every 5 to 8 days", not fetched, so NOT SOURCED as a split); Hany Rambod's own FST-7 five-day layout (muscleandbrawn.com returned HTTP 403, so only the search-result description exists, not a fetched page); Renaissance Periodization bodybuilding-specific "off-season" template (see B3).
